Uzbekistan

Karimov Discusses CIS Meeting, Tajik Elections,
Foreign Aid

LD2911212394 Tashkent Uzbekistan Television in
Russian 1635 GMT 29 Nov 94

[News conference held by President Islam Karimov for
members of the Tashkent diplomatic corps and corre-
spondents in the Palace of the Friendship of the Peoples
in Tashkent on 29 November—recorded]

{[FBIS Translated Excerpt] [Karimov] [passage omitted]
The third question I want to draw your attention to is the
questions connected to the meeting of CIS heads of states
that took place, as you know, on 21 October 1994.

This CIS meeting examined issues of the further deep-
ening of integration and also agreed measures to resolve
the problems that exist today. I will make use of today’s
presence of the diplomatic corps and representatives of
the press to express once again, to repeat, my attitude to
the principle questions, in our view, connected to the
prospects for the development of the CIS.

Unlike the supporters of the creation of various kinds of
new forms of associations proposing that work begin on
forming supra-state political structures and superstruc-
tures like the so-called Eurasian Union, we believe in
Uzbekistan that today there is no alternative to the CIS,
despite the weak points that can be noted in it, and that
it meets the national state interests of all its members.

A somewhat paradoxical—I would say incomprehensi-
ble—situation is forming where they want to lead the
public of the CIS, all CIS countries, into delusion. The
fact is that CIS countries that exist today in the post-
Soviet area are experiencing various degrees of crisis—
politically and economically. It is namely the disastrous
situation of people that sometimes creates nostalgia for
those times when the USSR existed. They want to use
this to create the imp.ession that at one time everything
was good, and now independent princes, as they say,
have appeared, independent principalities that are inca-
pable of dealing with their situation. In this sense various
forms of association are proposed that will supposedly
save the situation; namely, the creation of various supr-
astate political structures such as a single parliament, a
single citizenship, and various suprastate executive
structures. But they are given a certain decorative dem-
ocratic nature, and with this they want to lead everyone
who lives in the post-Soviet area into delusion.

CENTRAL ASIA 63

Tell me, please, if there is a single parliament, a single
citizenship, a single armed forces, what should this state
be called? And various, I would say, vagaries around this
issue have a single nature—and I will repeat it once
more—to bring ordinary people into delusion.

In our view integration has to begin with the economy. A
house, as I said at the CIS meeting in Moscow, a house is
not built starting with the roof; a house is built starting
with the foundations. And the foundations in all times
has been the economy. It is economic integration that
will create the prerequisites that will allow and ensure
integration in all other areas, first and foremost in
questions of culture, the spiritual sphere, and in ques-
tions .onnected as a whole with cooperation between the
states that today make up the CIS.

This tendency meets those decisions that were taken at
the CIS session, such as on the basic directions of CIS
integrational development, the prospective plan for the
integration and the development of the CIS, the creation
of a payments union and also the creation of an inter-
state economic committee. We believe that the forma-
tion of these structures is that beginning which will
ensure profound integration, first and foremost on eco-
nomic questions.

I would like to draw your attention to this fact—
everybody, when a lot is being said about the Eurasian
union, when they speak to their electorates and speak
about the need for integration, in deed do little so as to
implement their declarations with specific, practical
steps, The creation of an inter-state economic committee
must ensure economic integration and the implementa-
tion of the document adopted 18 months ago on the
economic union. More than a month has passed since
the decision was taken to create the economic com-
mittee, but no practical steps on starting the work of this
body are being taken. This latest decision may really
become the 401 st decision taken by the CIS that remains
on paper. [Word indistinct] to those who say a> awful lot
and loudly at all forums about integration but .n deed do
virtually nothing. In the solution of all these problems,
the role of Russia as a great power is very great, but in
practice Russia does not go beyond declarations. [pas-
sage omitted]

Uzbekistan greets the first shoots of the positive changes
that have appeared in Tajikistan. Confirmation of this,
in Our opinion, are the recent Tajik presidential elections
on a multi-party, competitive basis. At the same time,
together with the elections, as you know there was a
referendum on adopting the constitution. And the Tajik
people voted for its adoption.

I would also like to state today that it is too early to talk
about the stabilization of the political and economic
situation in Tajikistan. I would like, taking advantage of
the fact that ambassadors of great states are present here,
to draw attention to the fact that the forthcoming winter
or tht winter that has already started in Tajikistan
threatens the most serious tragedies for the people of

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

64 CENTRAL ASIA

Tajikistan. If we do not help and render humanitarian
aid to Tajikistan, the 5 million people of Tajikistan will
find it very difficult to survive this winter.

Taking the occasion of this meeting today, I would like to
say in regard to the giving of humanitarian aid by
international organizations and various financial inter-
national organizations that there is a tendency that is
incomprehensible to me. While rendering humanitarian
aid, if you analyze this situation on the example of
individual states, you will be convinced that humani-
tarian aid is, as a rule, rendered not to a suffering people
but taking into the account what political authority its
leaders enjoy in the world. And this is a paradox. You
will agree with me that humanitarian aid is designed not
for leaders and not for certain political systems, but
humanitarian aid, as humane aid, is designed for people
suffering great deprivations. [passage omitted]

WHO Representatives To Assist Health Care
Reforms

LD3011110694 Tashkent Radio Tashkent Network in
Uzbek 0600 GMT 29 Nov 94

[FBIS Translated Text] Representatives from the World
Health Organization [WHO] and specialists from © <r-
many, Romania, Britain, Mongolia, Russia, and other
states have begun carrying out a project of recommen-
dations and practical assistance for Central Asian med-
ical workers. The project envisages improving health
care provision, especially funding for this sector and
assistance in utilizing resources for the medical sector. A
package of measures to support important health care
projects in the Central Asian republics within the frame-
work of the Carenet program has been especially worked
out by the international health care organization and is
now being implemented.

The consultative conference, which has begun its work in
Tashkent is one such measure. Health ministers from a
number of states are taking part. The conference is
devoted to management of the medical sector and
funding for it.

The funding and management of health care in Uzbeki-
stan have their own specific characteristics, Health Min-
ister Shavkat Karimov said. They are pursued on the
basis of the legislation passed by parliament and the

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-94-230
30 November 1994

regulatory legal documents issued by the government.
The economic reforms, market relationships, and free
competition that are being implemented in our republic
are being introduced to the health sector as well. New
sources of funding are being sought and found. The
government provides free medical services within the
limits that it can back financially, but this is not gener-
ally enough to improve medical services.

Only if different kinds of paid medical services are
introduced can health care be raised to a high standard.
However, the money for treatment should not be paid by
the patient, but rather covered out of the funds of the
enterprise, establishment, or organization where the
patient works.

Ways of moving over to a medical insurance system
separate from the money the government allocates to
health care from the state budget are being worked out.
Uzbekistan’s Government was the first in the CIS to
issue a law on private treatment. The right to charge for
treatment has been granted to a number of medical
establishments and individual doctors recently. There
can be no doubt that this will lead to a raising of
standards in the medical services available to the popu-
lation. This is the experience of many developed states
such as Turkey, the United States of America, Germany,
as well as Russia and other CIS states. Uzbekistan’s
doctors are studying this experience too.

The visiting WHO representatives, particularly Konstan
Sakelaridas, the head of its European medical services
bureau, and Sardor Savish, the organization’s regional
adviser, are rendering invaluable assistance. During the
three-day conference the WHO experts will hold study
meetings with Central Asian medical workers. They will
exchange experiences and draft joint recommendations
for assistance in reforming the funding of medicine.

LD3011194094 Tashkent Radio Tashkent Network in
Uzbek 1230 GMT 29 Nov 94

[FBIS Translated Texi] Today, an expanded-format
meeting took place at the republican prosecutor’s office

lican laws on securing state pensions for citizens of the
republic. Uzbek Social Security Minister Bahodir Umir-
zogov, Rahmonberdi Zikirov, the chairman of the
Uzbekpochtasi [Uzbek postal service] concern, and
Akrom Nuritdinov, the deputy director of the social
security fund department under the Cabinet of Ministers
of Uzbekistan, reported on work done in their areas. The
prosecutors of the Karakalpak Republic, the regions, and
Tashkent city took part in the meeting. It was chaired by
Mustafayev, the prosecutor general of the republic.

The checks carried out by the prosecutor’s offices
showed that the implementation of the republican law
on providing state pensions for citizens of the republic
and of the governmental resolutions passed on 3 Sep-
tember 1993 and 10 January 1994 is unsatisfactory.
Monitoring of these laws and resolutions by the local
prosecution organizations is not at a high level. At
certain social security establishments, cases of embezzle-
ment of funds, criminal abuses, forgery, and bribery were
discovered. There is red tape in the processing of the
documents for allocating the benefits. The post office
workers are also paying less attention to their duties to
provide the pensions and pay them on time.

All such cases were criticized, and it was stressed that
cases of legal violations should be eliminated immedi-
ately they are identified, and monitoring by the prosecu-
tion service should be increased in order to meet legal
requirements on the ground and to press legal charges
against those who are guilty. Appropriate decisions were
taken on each issue considered at the meeting.

Abkhaz Leader Comments on Independence,
Negotiations

LD2911153594 Moscow INTERFAX in English 1340
GMT 29 Nov 94

[FBIS Transcribed Text] President Vladislav Ardzinba
of Abkhazia, the breakaway region of Georgia declaring
independence from Tbilisi on Saturday [26 November],
has expressed certainty that negotiations on a regional
settlement will not stop even if Abkhazia adopts a new
constitution.

Speaking in Sukhumi on Tuesday, he maintained that
the new constitution would make little change in the
status of Abkhazia which was a sovereign state under the
1925 constitution.

“While the 1925 constitution was based on principles
such as dictatorship of the proletariat and class struggle,
the new one meets all standards of international law”, he
stressed.

The president has described Georgian leader Eduard
Shevardnadze’s statement following the declaration of
Abkhaz independence as an emotional document not
befitting a statesman.

He called on the Georgian authorities to renounce an
ideology of aggressive nationalism and colonial thinking.

Ardzinba said his meeting with Shevardnadze scheduled
in Geneva on December 2 will be held later because of
inability by Russian Foreign Minister Andrey Kozyrev
to arrive in Switzerland on that day. “Eduard Shevard-
nadze has not refused to take part’, the Abkhaz leader
said.

30 November 1994

Adoption of New Abkhaz Constitution Viewed

MM2911160394 Moscow PRAVDA in Russian 29 Nov
94 pl

[Report by Igor Lenskiy: “Abkhazia Chooses Freedom.
Abkhaz Supreme Soviet Meets and Adopts New Consti-
tution Proclaiming a Republic and an Independent Sov-
ereign State”’]

{[FBIS Translated Text] The deputies unanimously
elected Vladislav Ardzinba the first president of Abk-
hazia and Sokrat Dzhindzholiya chairman of the
Supreme Soviet. Soon there will be a referendum on
confidence in the president.

The Supreme Soviet statement says that it is necessary to
continue the traditions of more than 1,000 years of
Abkhaz statehood. The previous constitution of 1925
has ceased to have any meaning, because it was based on
quite different realities and on a system of organs of state
power that no longer exists. At the same time, the
statement stresses that “Abkhazia is not breaking off the
negotiating process with Georgia and is prepared to
continue it with the aim of creating a union state with
two equal components.”

As you might expect, Tbilisi reacted extremely badly. In
an alarmist appeal to the world community E. Shevard-
nadze described the Abkhaz as “separatists” and called
for “effective measures within the framework of the UN
Charter, the CSCE, and the CIS” to be taken against
them.

Natella Akaba, parliamentary deputy and press minister,
told PRAVDA about the Supreme Soviet session on
Saturday [26 November]. In the presence of guests and
journalists, Vladislav Ardzinba, summing up the latest

CAUCASUS 69

Georgian-Abkhaz meeting in Geneva, said that the talks
were evidently headed in the direction of a confedera-
tion. And a confederation, as is known, cannot comprise
unequal parts. What we are talking about now is a union
of two states—independent and sovereign. So we have to
declare ourselves in a more definite form. According to
N. Akaba, Georgia, by its endless talk about “broad
autonomy” (which no one in Abkhazia takes seriously),
itself prompted the Abkhaz parliament to adopt a new
Constitution. Moreover, N. Akaba believes, if Georgia
refuses to form a confederation with Abkhazia for what-
ever reason, then it will be reasonable for Abkhazia and
Russia to establish confederative relations.

The Abkhaz parliament’s decisive step appears entirely
logical if you consider the results of the Georgian-
Abkhaz war: Georgia’s loss of control over Abkhazia in
this context is a form of responsibility for the armed
invasion of 14 August 1992.

A senior Russian Federation Foreign Ministry aide, who
wished to remain anonymous, decribed the Abkhaz
parliament’s move in conversation with your correspon-
dent as “extremely unexpected, leading to the removal of
Russia and the United Nations from the Geneva talks,
their possible breakdown, and a further exacerbation of
the conflict.” In the diplomat’s opinion, the Abkhaz are
unlikely to obtain international recognition and support.

Igor Akhba, Abkhaz ambassador to the Russian Federa-
tion, told PRAVDA that there was nothing sudden about
it—the draft has been under discussion for six months
and Russia knew what was going on. Nonetheless, the
meeting between V. Ardzinba and E. Shevardnadze, set
for 2 December under the auspices of the United
Nations, the CSCE, and the Russian Federation, will not
take place.

is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

Estonia

Baltic Defense Forces Pledge More Cooperation

LD3011105794 Vilnius Radio Vilnius Network in
Lithuanian 0800 GMT 30 Nov 94

[FBIS Translated Text] Estonia, Lithuania, and Latvia
are going to cooperate more closely in the military sphere
and will aspire to accelerate the integration into the
European military structures.

Aleksandr Einseln, Jonas Andriskevicius, and Juris Dar-
binis, the commanders of the military forces of the three
Baltic states were holding a conference in Tallinn. They
decided to set up a joint group that will coordinate their
actions according to the NATO program Partnership for
Peace. The group, which will be headed by the Estonian
Chief of Staff Arvo Sirel, will hold its first meeting on
16-17 January of next year.

Commanders of the military forces of the Baltic states
told the journalists that in spite of financial difficulties
the process of training and maintenance of the Baltic
peace-keeping battalion was proceeding without major
problems. The servicemen of the Baltic countries will
take part in the NATO maritime maneuvers, which will
be held next summer.

An agreement was signed in Tallinn to set up a working
group, which will prepare a common air space control
system of the three Baltic states. The role of voluntary
militarized organizations was also discussed.

[FBIS Transcribed Text] Russian deputy minister of

Medvedjev stated that the unilateral border demarcation

and Council of Europe when Estonia joined the organi-

against unilateral demarcation of Estonian-Russian

territory when joining the UN and Council of Europe.

the maps Estonia had stated that she had no territorial
claims against her neighbours, Leisson added.

huge costs unilaterally the joint border of the two states.

increasing crime in both countries.

expressed hope in the name of Russian president that
Estonian-Russian contacts, the summits among them,
would continue and the meeting of Presidents Meri and
Yeltsin in July did not remain a single meeting to
achieve one aim, i.e. Russian troop withdrawal from
Estonia.

Estonia Supports Chechen Right to
Self-Determination

LD2911185994 Moscow INTERFAX in English 1621
GMT 29 Nov 94

[FBIS Transcribed Text] Estonia supports the right of
the Chechen people to self-determination, Prime Min-
ister Andres Tarand told Interfax Tuesday [29
November].

He said that Estonia could not influence the world
politics to a great extent but “we were aided by the world
public opinion in our own fight for independence.”

self-determination of the peoples, notably the Chechen
people.

Environment Ministry Press Conference on Kiisa
Affair
WS2911134294 Tallinn ETA in English 0919 GMT 29
Nov 94

[From 29 Nov POSTIMEES, EESTI SONUMID, p. 3, 4;
“News Bulletin” No. 233/94]

[FBIS Transcribed Text] Kiisa affair showed clearly the
need for a modern and well-guarded storage of radioac-
tive materials in Estonia, Environment Ministry officials
expressed their opinion at yesterday's press conference.

Environment Minister Vootele Hansen admitted that
the storage building in Kiisa where the dangerously
radioactive piece of metal was stolen, was not up to
modern standards. The question of either building a new
storing place or modernizing the old one needs to be
resolved quite soon. He mentioned that the cost of the
new building could be as high as tens of milliards of
kroons.

The Head Specialist of the Institute of Meteorology and
Hydrography Henno Putnik considered best the idea of
storing the radioactive materials in Paldiski in future, as
the area of the Paldiski nuclear reactors would be under
a special guard for a long period anyway.

As the investigation of the Kiisa affair was still uncon-
cluded the ministry specialists were not able to inform
the journalist about the origins of the radioactive metal
or of possible charges against any persons. It was stressed
that a constant monitoring of the radiation level in the
country should be organized, providing the information
of any dangerous situations. In the Kiisa case the high
radiation was local and did not cause pollution in the
area.

Estonia would need international help, EESTI
SONUMID considers. Maybe even an international
investigation commission should be set up on the Kiisa
case.

BALTIC STATES 71

Soviet-Era Prime Minister Arrested for Giving
Bribes

WS3011120894 Tallinn BNS in English 1658 GMT 29
Nov 94

[FBIS Transcribed Text] Tallinn, Nov 29, BNS—
Estonian security police on Monday [28 November]
arrested Indrek Toome, head of the country’s last Soviet-
era government, after he had given 30,000 kroons
(USD2,400) in bribes to a security police official.

Toome, 51, wanted back the passports of three of what
he called his “business friends.” The passports had been
earlier seized on suspicions that their owners had ille-
gally obtained Estonian citizenship, the PAEVALEHT
newspaper reports.

Aivar Ild, who together with Toome owns the company
that bought one of Tallinn’s two largest hotels earlier this
year, told BNS on Tuesday Toome’s version of the
course of Mondays events was to be presented in a
special statement to the press, due later in the day.

The Tallinn Administrative Court ordered Toome under
arrest for 10 days. The former prime minister is cur-
rently being held at a police prison in Tallinn. He faces
charges that could be settled with a fine or send him to
prison for up to two years.

Security police while searching Toome’s home later on
Monday found a box of machinegun cartridges.

Chief of Police Investigation Department Priit Mannik
said the security police, who currently are in charge of
the investigation, within ten days had to hand Toome
over to his department and lay formal charges against
him.

The PAEVALEHT newspaper, quoting reliable sources,
said Toome some weeks ago had called the security
police official who was conducting investigations into
the passports affair and offered to meet privately over
the issue.

The official had informed his superiors.

The businessman turned former prime minister at a
meeting with the security police official later suggested
to “talk like a businessman to a businessman” and
offered to pay 30,000 kroons if the passports were
returned, PAEVALEHT says.

Other security police officials recorded and videotaped
contacts between Toome and the official. Toome on
Monday gave the official 30,000 kroons and received
three Estonian passports in return. The passports were
again seized in the presence of witnesses after Toome
was arrested at 4 p.m. on Monday.

The newspaper said the passports belonged to a Russian
businessman living in Tallinn, his wife and grown-up
daughter, respectively. “he man allegedly had faked data
about the place of birth of his mother, thus obtaining

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

72 BALTIC STATES

Estonian citizenship for the whole family. The man was
said to be a client of a company which Toome co-owns.

Interior Minister Kaido Kama in his comments said he
was glad that there still were honest state officials in
Estonia who turned down bribes. The minister called the
incident a “precedent that cleans the society and makes
it more healthy.”

The RAHVA HAAL newspaper said that in arresting
Toome the country’s police was launching a decisive
battle against corruption.

Prime Minister Andres Tarand in his comments to BNS
called Toome’s alleged action regrettable, particularly as
“Toome was considered to be the most pro-Estonian” in
the country’s former top Communist leadership.“ "This
indicates that Estonia’s moral rebirth is only possible
with new people,“ Tarand said.

“The law is one for everybody and everybody will be
punished according to law,” the prime minister said.

Toome’s business partner from Finland, Ilppo Kokkila,
said he had always known Toome as a man of high
morals. “Therefore it is very difficult for me to believe
that such thing has happened,” Kokkila, chairman of
SRV Viitoset, said.

“If it really was like the Estonian press is presenting it, it
was a very thoughtless step by Toome,” the business
partner said.

Kokkila said Toome’s arrest was not to affect the SRV
Group’s business plans in Estonia, including the
financing of the Viru Hotel.

Toome and Ild own the company Harmaron Ltd. which
bought the Viru Hotel from the state under the privati-
zation process in spring this year. The deal was financed
by Kokkila’s company.

Toome worked in the real estate company SRV Kinnis-
vara, partly owned by Kokila.

Toome was the country’s prime minister beween 1988
and 1990. He was member of the parliament when
Estonia formally became independent from Moscow in
1991. His earlier career included positions as head of the
local Communist party organization in the country’s
second largest town, Tartu, since 1978, and as the Soviet
Republic’s deputy prime minister in 1984-88.

Finance Minister Comments on 1995 Budget Plan

WS3011122594 Tallinn BNS in English 1805 GMT 29
Nov 94

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] Riga, Nov 29, BNS—The
Latvian parliament will start to consider the 1995 draft
budget plan this week, outlining the volumes of the basic
budget, local budgets and special budgets, Finance Min-
ister Andris Piebalgs told reporters Tuesday [29
November].

The planned income of the basic budget is 475 million
lats and the expenses—515 million lats, thus, the maxi-
mally admissible budget deficit is 40 million lats.

Commenting on budget priorities, Piebaigs said that the
largest specific weight of spendings is in the education
sphere making up 21.1 percent of the totally planned
expenditure, or 108.8 million lats.

In 1994, the budget allotted 83 million lats for educa-
tional needs.

The planned expenditure of the local governments lev-
eling fund constitutes 12.6 percent of total spendings,
12.5 percent is earmarked for health care, 11.4 percent
for law enforcement, 9.1 for economy, 7.5 percent for
welfare allowances, 7.1 percent for state administration,
5.5 percent of state internal obligations, and 4.6 percent
for security and defense.

The wage fund is increased 30.1 percent in the new
budget plan compared to the 1994 budget, said Piebalgs.

Speaking on state income, Piebalgs said the largest
revenues are planned from the sales tax and excise tax
collection. In addition, part of alcohol and tobacco
excise income will be channeled to finance health care.

The 1995 state budget will not include the Bank of
Latvia’s profit remainder, which was 7.9 million lats this
year.

Regarding local budgets, the Finance Ministry took into
account the municipal income leveling mechanism that
was created by the State Reform Ministry and the
Latvian Local Governments Association.

Consequently, city budgets will receive 100 percent of
residents’ income tax payments, rural district budgets
will receive 85 percent of the sum, while regional budgets
will have to put up with 15 percent.

Seventeen local governments of cities, including Riga,
Ventspils, Liepaja, will transfer resources to the local
governments leveling fund. The deductions, however,
should not exceed 45 percent of residents’ income tax
money. The other local governments will receive state
budget subsidies. The total income of local governments

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-94-230
30 November 1994

is planned at 150 million lats. The additional state
budget subsidies are projected at 62.7 million lats.

The special budget plan includes the social budget, the
privatization fund, the state property fund, the forestry
fund and the roads fund. The social budget, which is one
of the most important special budgets, is planned at 269
million lats in 1995. This budget will include social tax
payments and state budget subsidies and spend its
resources to pay allowances, pensions and the state social
insurance fund.

The 1995 budget plan was drafted in compliance with
the existent tax legislation but the adoption of the
package of new taxation laws will cause little changes in
it, said Piebalgs. Piebalgs said he hoped that the parlia-
ment would pass the draft budget plan already this year.

LD2911230694 Riga Radio Riga Network in Latvian
2100 GMT 29 Nov 94

[FBIS Translated Text] [Announcer] The government
session on Tuesday [29 November] approved the draft
state budget for 1995. The revenue part of the budget is
475 million lats; the expenditure part is 515 million. The
maximum permissible budget deficit is set at 40 million
lats. Minister of Finance Andris Piebalgs at a news
conference on Tuesday spoke about next year’s state
budget:

[Begin Piebalgs recording] The overall state budget con-
sists of three part; the first concerns local government
budgets. Of course, with the Association of Local
Authorities, we had differences of opinion too, as is
known.

Firstly, about what we actually agreed on: The prognoses
of both the Association of Local Authorities and the
Ministry of Finance are very close about these revenues
next year: 150 million lats. The Ministry of Finance is
offering to transfer a state subsidy of 62.7 million lats to
the local government compensation fund, which corre-
sponds to the overa'l growth in the budget that we have
for next year, and even a bit more. The Local Govern-
ment Association requested 296.6 million lats, although
differences of opinion remained here too; however, the
Association understands that at the moment such a sum,
intended for direct subsidies, is simply not possible for
us. A scheme for compensation has been worked out,
intended either as grants or to be transferred as appro-
priate to the local government compensation fund from
several local authorities.

The transfer to the local government budget compensa-
tion fund must not be greater than 45 percent of the
income tax recoverable from the authority’s residents.
For 17 local authorities a percentage of income tax from
residents is simply to be transferred to the compensation
fund, and all the rest get corresponding subsidies.

The second part is special budgets. The biggest of these
special budgets are the social budget - 269 million lats;

but these are also shown as special budgets, and they are
serviced by the state treasury, but, as I repeat again, these
special budgets have their indicated revenues and indi-
cated expenditures. So here the Saeima can decide on
them in the framework of this budget, but they cannot be

Committee Passes Security Police Budget for 1995

WS$2911105494 Tallinn BNS in English 1815 GMT 28
Nov 94

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] Riga, Nov 28, BNS—The
Latvian Saeima (parliament) National Security Com-
mittee approved Monday [28 November] the 1995
budget plan of security police and Home Guard infor-
mation service. Latvia’s secret services will spend
around 3 million lats next year, according to the draft
budget.

The committee faced problems with consideration of the
Defense Ministry counterintelligence service budget
because Minister Janis Trapans doubted the purposefull-
ness of the budget consideration at the committee, said
committee Chairman Andrejs Pantelejevs.

Under the law, the only control over secret services is
confirmation of their budgets and control of the use of
budget resources, said Pantelejevs.

Sea Border ‘Compromise’ With Latvia Confirmed

W'S3011121394 Tallinn BNS in English 1658 GMT 29
Nov 94

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] Vilnius, Nov 29, BNS—
Lithuanian Prime Minister Adolfas Slezevicius con-
firmed that an agreement on sea border between
Lithuania and Latvia was reached during his meeting
last Friday [25 November] with Latvian Prime Minister
Maris Gailis.

At a press conference in Vilnius Tuesday, Slezevicius
called the agreement “compromise,” but ‘efused to
reveal its essence, saying the sides had agreed not to
announce any information while talks with Russia are in
_ progress.

Slezevicius said it was important to show the world that
two neighboring Baltic countries are able to agree, and he
says this is more important than “‘! or 2 km in the sea
100 km from the shore.” There had to be will from both
sides to resolve the problem, he added.

The Lithuanian premier noted, however, that the
opinion of parliamentary factions will be taken into
consideration and no hasty decisions will be made.

Referring to criticism expressed by opposition leader
Vytautas Landsbergis, the prime minister said superpa-
triotism should be separated from sensible politics.
Slezevicius said he cannot deny Landsbergis’ statements
because the opposition leader does not have any official
information

Landsbergis alleged Monday that the agreement between
premiers limited the boundary between Lithuanian and
Swedish economic zones to one nautical mile.

Slezevicius, Matochkin Discuss Border, Transit,
Trade

WS3011121794 Tallinn BNS in English 1805 GMT 29
Nov 94

[FBIS Transcribed Text] Vilnius, Nov 29, BNS—
Lithuanian Prime Minister Adolfas Slezevicius and the
head of the Kaliningrad region’s administration, Yuri
Matochkin, agreed that experts would meet Saturday to
discuss border crossing procedures, the rules of escorting
transit cargos and other issues related to Russian transit
through Lithuania.

Slezevicius and Matochkin met in Vilnius Monday [28
November]. The talks focused on transit, borders and
trade, the Lithuanian premier told reporters Tuesday.
““Matochkin has been given quite big authorizations by
Russian Prime Minster Viktor Chernomyrdin,” he said.

Referring to the meeting, the Lithuanian premier said he
reminded Matochkin that Russia’s economic pressure is
a disadvantage not only to Lithuania, but to Russia, as
well. Or the other hand, he said, smaller trade volumes
make Lithu2nia less dependent on Russia, “‘and such is
our aspiration.”

As an example, Slezevicius indicated that Lithuania
intends to import light oil products through the Klaipeda
port and to build a high voltage line to import Western
electricity via Poland.

Asked whether Lithuania should continue talks with
Russia and Kaliningrad until the Lithuanian-Russian
agreements signed earlier take effect, Slezevicius said
Lithuania should not stop the negotiating process. “We
must use even the slightest opportunity to agree with all
of our neighbors.”

Minister Discusses Russian Trade, Kaliningrad

WS$2911151794 Kaliningrad SVOBODNAYA ZONA in
Russian 17 Nov 94 p 2

[“Exclusive” interview with Industry and Trade Min-
ister Kazimieras Klimasauskas by Irina Shevchenko;
place and date not given: “Kazimieras Klimasauskas:
‘The Goal Is One—To Increase Economic Potential’’’]

[FBIS Translated Text] Russia and Lithuania have tra-
ditionally had good-neighborly re'ations, as confirmed
by the diverse and close ties in economics and culture, as
well as in. many other areas. As a result of the collapse of
the Soviet Union—a state system that existed until just
recently—many contacts were disrupted. However, a
need to restore and expand them has currently become
obvious to both Lithuania and Russia, and in particular,
Kaliningrad Oblast.

A SVOBODNAYA ZONA correspondent devoted his
meeting with Kazimieras Klimasauskas, minister of
trade and industry of the Republic of Lithuania, to the
state of affairs in our cooperation and prospects for its
development.

[Shevchenko] Mr. Minister, what effect has the disrup-
tion of the former ties between our two states had?

{Klimasauskas] It primarily affected our relations.
Whereas in the past our trade with Russia represented
two thirds of the total volume of our trade, that is 60
percent, in the third quarter of 1994 it merely amounted
to one third of our total trade turnover, that is 33
percent. You must agree that these figures are very
revealing. Germany now comes in second, and is fol-
lowed by Latvia, Poland, and the Netherlands. Belarus
and Ukraine, which once followed Russia in second and
third places respectively, are now in sixth and seventh.

Our exports to Russia has decreased 2.5-fold, and this is
particularly evident in light industry: Whereas it totalled
$20.7 million in the second quarter of 1993, in the
corresponding period of this year it amounted to a mere

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-94-230
30 November 1994

$9.6 million. The situation with our export of light
industrial products to Europe is quite different: In the
second quarter of 1993 it amounted to $7.2 million, and
in the second quarter of 1994 it was almost $22 million.
The complications concerning our trade and economic
relations with Russia are forcing us to become oriented
toward Western markets. We are expecting a 10- or
15-percent increase in our foreign trade in 1994. Last
year it amounted to $3.9 billion. Our exports are con-
tinuing to increase, and we predict that they should reach
$4 billion in 1994.

[Shevchenko] Is Lithuania interested in business coopera-
tion with Russia and, in particular, our oblast?

[Klimasauskas] Actually, our relations with Kaliningrad
Oblast have not been disrupted. I stand for the develop-
ment of joint venture companies on your territory, since I
consider this the beginning of integration that cannot be
hampered by any borders. Kaliningrad Oblast’s trade with
our country has reached $1.618 million this year, which is
4.1 times higher than last year’s figure. Construction
materials represent a significant portion of Lithuanian
exports, and over 40 Lithuanian construction companies
are currently operating ia the city of Kaliningrad alone. In
our opinion, joint ventures in other industrial branches
also have great prospects.

There are no doubts that only through economic ties will
we be able to overcome the complex economic situation
which has developed in our countries.

We welcome the decision by the leadership of the Kalin-
ingrad Oblast Administration to draft a program for coop-
eration between Kaliningrad Oblast and the Lithuanian
Republic, and to develop mechanisms for organizational
and economic cooperation.

Such a program may become an efficient means, if it is
drafted in cooperation with the Lithuanian side.

Prime Minister Slezevicius on EU, U.S. Talks
LD2911165994

[FBIS Editorial Report] Vilnius Radio Vilnius Network in
Lithuanian at 1420 GMT on 29 November carries a report
by Zenonas Miksys from the prime minister’s news con-
ference. Miksys reports that the conference started with
the issue of Lithuania’s relations with the European
Union. Prime Minister Slezevicius says:

“The European Union has been given a mandate for
negotiations with Lithuania, Latvia, and Estonia on
drafting the treaty on associated membership. This is no
doubt one of the most important events in our relations
with the European Union and we are very glad that the
dialogue is proceeding according to the schedule that was

BALTIC STATES 75

set up. A visit to Brussels, the headquarters of NATO, by
our delegation in order to achieve final coordination of the
Partnership for Peace program is also of great importance.

“It was emphasised during the talks with Secretary of State
Warren Christopher and his deputy, Strobe Talbott, that in
rendering assistance to the Baltic peacekeeping unit which
is to start as of the beginning of January next year, it is of
great importance that after signing the treaty on Partner-
ship for Peace, Lithuania and other neighboring countries
should not forget their obligations and give support with a
concrete program.”

The prime minister also spoke about his visit to the United
States. Slezevicius says:

“It was very important for us to hear the point of view of
the U.S. Administration and government officials on the
main three issues of our foreign policy. Those are: the
problem of military transit, the economic policy pursued
by Russia toward the Baltic states, and policy toward
NATO. The stance of Lithuania as well as other neigh-
boring states on the above issues was given unreserved
support, which no doubt gives us much hope. These issues
will be discussed and resolved positively at the summit,
which is to take place in December.”

The prime minister also spoke about the possible credits
from American banks to Lithuanian private enterprises.

Talking about his meeting with Latvian Prime Minister
Maris Gailis, he said that the main issue under discussion
was the maritime border deliniation. With Polish Prime
Minister Pawlak, he discussed the project of the Via
Baltica motorway, the construction of a railway ef Euro-
pean standard, and of high tension power supply lines.

Asked by journalists about his meeting with the leader of
the Kaliningrad region, Marochkin, Slezevicius said that
they discussed supplies to the Kaliningrad region, transit,
and other issues connected with it.

Lines Extend Several Kilometers at Border With
Poland

WS2911150994 Tallinn BNS in English 1300 GMT 29
Nov 94

[FBIS Transcribed Text] Vilnius, Nov 29, BNS—Tensions
began mounting as the line of vehicles extended to several
kilometers over the past several days at the Lazdijai border
post in Lithuania. Around 100 trucks and 200 cars were
reported waiting to cross the Lithuanian-Polish border
Monday [28 November] before noon.

Around 90 trucks and 150 to 200 cars are allowed to pass
through a day, meaning that those waiting at the end of the
line would have to wait for at least 24 hours to cross the
border.

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

76 BALTIC STATES

Lithuanian customs officials blame the disorder on the
Polish side, which they say introduced new work rules
about a week ago. Lithuanian officials said the Poles allow
vehicles to enter their zone from only one line, although
there are four, and only during daytime.

Lithuanian customs officials began redirecting trucks to
the Kalvarijos post, about 50 kilometers from Lazdijai. No
lines were reported at Kalvarijos Monday, however the
post accepts only cargo transport.

FBIS-SOV-94-230
30 November 1994

Lazdijai officials said serious conflicts have been avoided,
except an incident Saturday, when angry truck drivers
blocked off traffic on the Polish side of the post. The
protest ended when police interfered.

The are no lines to enter Lithuania from Poland.
Lithuanian customs officials claim they are unable to
convince their Polish colleagues to change the new rules
and say the misunderstanding should be settled on govern-
ment level.
